# Data inventory

Generated by `scripts/maintenance/inventory_datasets.py` (re-runnable; reads metadata only).
**54 GB across 8,739 files.** Detailed per-file listing follows the summary.

## What this data is, and where it came from

The repository began as a *solar–magnetic* study (does solar activity modulate the polar
vortex and hence surface hazard?). That hypothesis was abandoned, but its data was kept.
**The result is that ~89% of the bytes have no bearing on the current SSW–avalanche paper.**

| group | size | what it physically is | provenance | relevance to the current paper |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| POES MEPED/TED particle flux | **27.3 GB** | NOAA-15/18 satellite energetic electron & proton flux, 2013–2025 | `09_download_poes_meped.py`, `21_…_fixed.py`, `44_…_hapi_historical.py` | **none** — legacy solar-precipitation hypothesis |
| Parker Solar Probe / GOES / OMNI | **16.6 GB** | PSP magnetometer CDFs, GOES X-ray & particle, solar-wind | `13_setup_psp_sdo.py`, `23`, `43`, `48`, `02_download_omni.py` | **none** — legacy solar hypothesis |
| Aura MLS O₃ + Temperature | **5.1 GB** | L3 daily zonal-mean stratospheric ozone and temperature, 2005–2025 | `11_setup_nasa_earthdata.py`, `50_redownload_mls.py` | **yes** — independent stratospheric verification of SSW warming (used in R92) |
| ERA5 polar stratosphere (gridded) | **2.3 GB** | `t,u,v,z` on pressure levels, **1979–2014 only** | `47_download_era5_polar_strat.py` | partial — *does not cover* the 2018/2019/2021/2023 SSWs |
| MERRA-2 polar stratosphere | 93 MB | reanalysis cross-check | `39_download_nasa_earthdata.py` | supporting |
| SNOTEL daily | 80 MB | 945 US mountain stations, SWE/precip/temp, 1980–2026 | `07`/`17_download_snotel*.py` | **yes** — independent US snowpack forcing (R102) |
| NCEP stratosphere | small | 10 hPa 60°N zonal wind, 1979–2024 | `27_download_ncep_stratosphere.py` | **yes** — SSW index |
| **cryosphere (all avalanche data)** | **446 MB** | see below | many | **this is the entire evidence base** |

The avalanche evidence base is **0.8% of the repository by size.**

## Every avalanche dataset, and what it can and cannot test

| dataset | records | coverage | observable type | usable for natural-release test? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `davos_avalanches/daily_activity.csv` | 3,533 d | 1998–2019 | **AAI split by trigger** (natural / human / explosive) | **YES — the only true natural-activity record** |
| `davos_avalanches/avalanche_observations.csv` | 13,918 | 1998–2019 | individual avalanches w/ trigger type | yes (same region) |
| `processed/cryosphere/norway_avalanche.parquet` | 53,907 bulletins, 65 regions | 2013–2026 | **structured avalanche PROBLEMS** (weak-layer cause, trigger sensitivity, avalanche type) | **partly — forecaster-assessed, not observed counts** |
| `lawis_full/incidents_full.csv` | 4,859 | 1992–2026 | accidents/incidents (11 countries) | no — human-exposure driven |
| `caic/caic_accident_data.xlsx` | 1,016 | 1951–2025 | US accidents | no — human-exposure driven |
| `uac_utah/…incidents_all.csv` | 8,205 | 2012–2026 | US accidents + avalanche problem | no |
| `envidat/weather_snowpack_danger.csv` | 292,837 station-d | 1997–2020 | SNOWPACK model output + danger | model, not observation |
| `albina/albina_danger.json` | 47,004 | AT/IT regions | danger level only | no |
| `us_danger_ratings_all.csv` | 69,871 | 2011–2025 | danger level only | no |
| `norway_nve/nve_danger_all.csv` | 22,848 | 2018–2025 | danger level only | no |
| `european_alps/data_dmax.csv` | 142,465 | **2011–2015 only** | danger level, multi-country | no |
| `envidat/swiss_danger_*.csv` | ~45,000 | 2001–2024 | danger level | no |
| `eaws_bulletins/` | 21 files | **one SSW window (2018)** | full bulletins incl. problems | too sparse |

### The key structural fact
Of ~600,000 avalanche-related records, **exactly one region (Davos) has observed natural
avalanche activity counts.** Everything else is either (a) a *danger rating* — a forecaster's
prediction, not an observation; (b) an *accident* — driven by how many people were out; or
(c) *model output*. Norway's problem archive is the closest independent substitute and is
tested in R109, but it covers only four canonical SSWs.

This is why the multi-region replication in R107 could not be a clean test of the
suppression hypothesis: the observable does not exist outside Davos in the data available.
